### Scenario 3 (continued): scope-bounded manual polling-loop soak driver (job-poll-worker.sh, item 8 -- explicitly NOT a production scheduler, see its own header) against a smaller dedicated 300-job batch, 3 concurrent worker processes, one worker deliberately failing every 5th job to exercise the retry/backoff/dead-letter path under load too ###
INSERT 0 300
worker=soak-worker-1 claimed=100 completed=100 failed=0 empty_polls=50
worker=soak-worker-2 claimed=100 completed=80 failed=20 empty_polls=50
worker=soak-worker-3 claimed=100 completed=100 failed=0 empty_polls=50
soak batch (300 jobs): completed=280 dead_letter=0 still_pending_retry=20 (deliberate failures schedule a real backoff retry -- not every failed job reaches dead_letter within this soak window, disclosed not a bug)

### Scenario 3 (continued): deterministic dead-letter + replay reconciliation (CG-S10-ATW-024 fix-pass addition, adversarial review -- Prompt 243 section 25/28d names 'reconcile ... replayed counts' and 'job retry/DLQ/replay reconciliation' as required; the soak batch above discloses dead_letter=0 within its own soak window by design, so the DLQ->replay path was never actually exercised end to end. This scenario forces it deterministically via max_attempts=1 (dead-letters on the very first recorded failure, no backoff wait needed) and the real app.requeue_dead_letter_job replay RPC, then proves every replayed job actually completes) ###
-- step 1: claim + deliberately fail each one (max_attempts=1 means the very first app.record_job_failure call dead-letters it, no backoff wait) --
DO
dead_lettered=10 (expected 10)
-- step 2: replay every dead-lettered job via the real app.requeue_dead_letter_job RPC --
DO
replayed_to_pending=10 (expected 10)
-- step 3: a real worker claims and completes every replayed job --
DO
completed_after_replay=10 still_dead_letter=0
SCENARIO 3d RESULT: PASS -- all 10 jobs genuinely reached dead_letter, were replayed via app.requeue_dead_letter_job, and completed -- reconciled accepted/dead-lettered/replayed/completed counts all match

### Scenario 7: restart/recovery (real client kill mid-transaction + real Postgres cluster restart) ###
== step 1: a real, fully-committed movement (baseline for the idempotent-retry proof) ==
committed movement id: 96f66130-e1ac-4274-9a5e-4a5eed16b0db
== step 2: an in-flight (never-committed) transaction, killed mid-transaction (SIGKILL on the psql client process, genuinely idle-in-transaction server-side -- not a server-side pg_sleep, which would keep running regardless of client liveness) ==
86223298-f8f6-42c9-ad05-351db1dc81be
PASS: killed in-flight transaction left zero committed rows (clean rollback) -- 0 rows for key idem-loadtest-recovery-inflight-11668
== step 3: restart the actual local Postgres cluster process (real server-level recovery, not merely a client reconnect) ==
PASS: Postgres cluster restarted and is accepting connections again
== step 4: post-restart durability -- the committed row from step 1 survived, the on_hand value is unchanged and consistent, the never-committed row from step 2 is still absent ==
PASS: on_hand unchanged across restart (before=991266, after=991266)
PASS: never-committed row still absent after restart (0 rows)
